[2026-10-25 02:10 IST] [Qoder] [PM-01R] [providerCredentialRef SOURCE — RUNTIME STRING, REF NOT RECOVERABLE]
Value-free follow-up to `{ok:false,reason:"inline"}`: the live config file still stores zero-gw `apiKey` as a SecretRef **object** (`source`/`provider`/`id`; env; uppercase id; value never read), but the plugin handler saw a **string** and hit the `typeof apiKey === "string"` branch. Host 2026.10.6 contract: `OpenClawPluginApi` exposes `config` and `runtime.config.current()` (runtime snapshot); there is **no plugin accessor** for the source/raw snapshot (`getRuntimeConfigSourceSnapshot` is internal), and `config.get` is redacted (`redactConfigSnapshot`). The runtime snapshot is published after secrets activation, so the string must be treated as resolved/opaque — exact nature not inspected (forbidden).
Conclusion: the original `{source,provider,id}` is **not recoverable from the supported plugin runtime config**. Keep `providerCredentialRef` fail-closed. **OpenCode remedy:** persist the non-secret ref identity at provider create/update (plugin keyed store, alongside display names), read that for `providerCredentialRef`; return `unknown`/`not-recorded` for providers that predate it (including zero-gw) and keep the same-host fallback. Do not reconstruct from runtime values, host conventions or `secrets.store.list` alone.
No set/delete, config write, secret read, provider call or restart. Gateway PID 19764, config sha `ad1536d86a9ef1de` unchanged. PM-01 stays READY FOR REVIEW; owner decision: accept fail-closed/UI fallback or authorize the persistence method fix, plus the orphan `ZERO_PROVIDER_ZERO_GW_API_KEY` decision.

[2026-10-25 02:20 IST] [Qoder] [PM-01R] [CREDENTIAL-REF IDENTITY CONTRACT — OPENCODE IMPLEMENTATION]
Qoder here: this pass is app-lane, so I did **not** edit `app/` or add UI tests. Backend contract verified for OpenCode:
- **Identity source:** `secrets.store.set {name,value,kind:"secret",allowedHosts?}` returns only `{ok,reloaded,warningCount}`; it does **not** echo the ref. Exact identity comes from the request: `{source:"store", provider:"default", id:<uppercase name>}`; env uses the exact supplied `{source:"env",provider,id}`. Persist only `{source,provider,id}` + `updatedAt`; never the value.
- **Ordering:** (1) secret write only when a new/replacement key is supplied; (2) `config.patch` with the provider; require `ok:true` and the expected provider path in `changedPaths` (response `config` is redacted, not an identity source); (3) only then persist metadata in a dedicated keyed-store namespace. On a failed patch, delete a **newly created** store name as compensation, never a replacement. On metadata failure, config+secret are live: report partial, retry metadata idempotently only. Key-omitted edit: omit `apiKey`, preserve the recorded identity.
- **Legacy/redacted:** getter returns `{ok:false,reason:"not-recorded"}` when no row exists; keep the same-host fail-closed fallback. Do not migrate/rewrite zero-gw or touch `ZERO_PROVIDER_ZERO_GW_API_KEY`.
- **Method contract:** getter reads the persisted row first; add `zero.ui.providerCredentialRef.set {providerId, ref}` and `.delete {providerId}` (closed/validated, metadata only). Registration needs a later Gateway restart; offline tests can land now.
Offline test matrix: new-provider save; credential replacement; key-omitted edit; failed config patch; failed metadata write/partial retry; legacy unknown identity; zero-gw/orphan untouched. Full contract: `reports/backend/control-ui-backend-contract.md` "Credential-reference identity persistence contract". PM-01 stays READY FOR REVIEW; no config/secret/provider call or restart in this pass.

[2026-10-25 02:30 IST] [Qoder] [PM-01R] [FAILED-PATCH SECRET RULE — NO AUTO-DELETE]
Confirmed the store contract: `secrets.store.set` is an upsert by name and returns only `{ok,reloaded,warningCount}`; it does **not** say whether the write created or replaced an entry. A pre-write existence check is not proof, and the fixed `ZERO_PROVIDER_<ID>_API_KEY` name may already exist. Therefore the identity-persistence contract now says: **never auto-delete a store entry after a failed config patch unless the implementation can prove the name was previously absent and not overwritten** (the current fixed-name flow cannot). On failure: leave the credential untouched, report `partial: secret-written, config-not-committed` with the name only (never the value), and offer an idempotent retry that re-patches the same exact identity. Metadata failure after config success stays `identity-pending`/`not-recorded` with idempotent metadata retry. Contract updated in `reports/backend/control-ui-backend-contract.md`; OpenCode can proceed with offline implementation/tests without further owner approval. Qoder did not edit `app/` or mutate live config/secrets; PM-01 stays READY FOR REVIEW.

[2026-10-25 03:32 IST] [OpenCode] [PM-01] [CREDENTIAL-REF IDENTITY PERSISTENCE — IMPLEMENTED, OFFLINE-VERIFIED]
Implemented Qoder's finalized contract in `app/`; no live config/secret write, provider call, Discover, reload/restart, or `zero-gw` migration.

**Runtime (source-wired; activation pending).** `runtime-core.mjs` now registers six methods: the three
display-name methods, `zero.ui.providerCredentialRef` (getter), `.set`, and `.delete`. Credential identity
rows live in a second retained namespace, `provider-credential-refs`, and hold only
`{source, provider, id, updatedAt}`. The getter reads only that row; absent or invalid rows return
`{ok:false, reason:"not-recorded"}`, and nothing is inferred from runtime config, host scope,
`secrets.store.list`, or values. `set` is closed to exactly `{source, provider, id}` with env/store
grammar and rejects the redaction marker. `zero-gw`/`ZERO_PROVIDER_ZERO_GW_API_KEY` are untouched.

**Save flow.** Identity comes only from the request (`store/default/<uppercase name>` or a staged
discovery name) and is persisted only after `config.patch` commits; a present `changedPaths` must include
`models.providers.<id>`. Ordering is secret (only when a new key is typed) → patch → identity metadata.
A failed patch never deletes the secret: the UI reports
`secret-written, config-not-committed: <name>` (name only) and offers an idempotent `Retry provider save`
that re-patches the same object and then records the same identity. A failed identity write after config
success reports `identity record is pending` with `Retry identity record`. Key-omitted edits omit
`apiKey` and preserve the recorded identity. No value enters pending state, metadata, notifications, or
the DOM.

[2026-10-25 03:53 IST] [OpenCode] [PM-01] [SENIOR-REVIEW CORRECTIONS — CANCEL RETENTION + STRICT changedPaths]
Senior review found two blocking mismatches; both are fixed in `app/` with focused regression tests. No
live config/secret write, provider call, or Gateway reload/restart in this pass.

**Cancel after staging (no deletion).** Removed the staged-secret deletion helper and the read-only
`secrets.store.list` probe used to claim freshness. `secrets.store.set` is an upsert, so neither can prove
this form originated the entry or that it was not replaced concurrently. Cancel now leaves the entry
untouched and shows a name-only notice: `Provider not saved. Staged credential <NAME> remains in the
Gateway secret store and is not bound to any provider. It was left untouched.` A confirmed save or
confirmed retry clears the staged marker before closing, so it does not show a false notice. No value is
read, stored, or displayed.

**Strict `changedPaths` gate.** A shared `assertProviderPathChanged` is applied to both the initial save
and `Retry provider save`. It requires `changedPaths` to be an array containing the exact
`models.providers.<id>`. Missing, non-array, or unrelated values throw `provider-path-unconfirmed`, so no
identity metadata and no success message are produced; the credential is never deleted, and an
idempotent config-retry state is preserved. With a secret the message is `secret-written,
config-unconfirmed: <name>`; without one it is `config-unconfirmed`. A thrown patch keeps the prior
`secret-written, config-not-committed` state. An unconfirmed retry keeps the recovery state and reports
`Retry failed`; a confirmed retry persists the exact identity and succeeds.

**Evidence.** `node --test app/zero-control-ui/test/mount.test.mjs` → **32/32 pass**. The preview-cancel

derived from the patched provider. Build → generation
`ff67c8460d64771d1739fda6b074ea073ec1a8f34a447feced297585a77ede2e` (JS 105,383 B, CSS 27,639 B),

[2026-10-25 20:47 IST] [OpenCode] [UI-05] [DELEGATION TRACE + OFFLINE CONTRACT]
Traced the existing Zero-to-worker path without sending a model prompt, calling a provider, or triggering
a computer action. The UI remains one `/chat/zero` conversation; Researcher/Operator stay backend workers.

**Live config trace (read-only).** `zero.subagents.allowAgents = ["researcher","operator"]`,
`requireAgentId:true`; `researcher.subagents.allowAgents = []`; `operator.subagents.allowAgents = []`.
Tool perimeters: Zero `coding`/`exec.ask`; Researcher denies write/edit/patch/exec/process/browser/
computer; Operator allows `computer` with `exec.deny`.

**Orchestration.** Zero calls `sessions_spawn` with an explicit worker id and a hidden one-shot run
(`visible:false`, `mode:"run"`, `context:"isolated"`, `expectsCompletionMessage:true`,
`completionTarget:"parent"`). The Gateway returns `{status:"accepted",runId,childSessionKey}`; the child
session is parented to Zero. Zero may call `sessions_yield`; completion (`ok|error|timeout|unknown` plus
the child result) is delivered as an internal requester turn to the original Zero session (WebChat
in-session, `deliver=false`), and Zero synthesizes the answer there. No specialist route/session link is
created.

**New offline tests.** `app/zero-control-ui/test/delegation.test.mjs` (4 tests, test-only harness over the
documented existing APIs; no product routing/message bus):
- research intent → `researcher`, computer intent → `operator`, both hidden and parented to Zero, results
  return to the same Zero session;
- child `error` returns without false success or a second top-level chat;
- child `timeout` returns as timeout and a retry uses a new hidden run in the same conversation;
- missing target, self-spawn, recursive worker spawn, and `visible:true` are rejected.
Full UI suite **49/49 pass** (45 surface + 4 delegation); bridge **3/3**. Generation/revision unchanged:
`bc41578…` / `12355b73…`.

FOLLOW-UP - 2026-10-26 15:30:00 +05:30 - OpenCode (UI/computer lane)

B5 SEAM AUDIT: HOST-OWNED, NO CODE CHANGE

  Decision: do not add a Zero chat-scroll helper. The installed
  ControlUiHost (apiVersion 1, openclaw 2026.10.6) exposes
  sessions/agents/navigation/ui/components/request/onEvent/subscribe, but no
  chat, transcript, or scroll method. onEvent is Gateway events, not DOM
  scroll. The only transcript hook is ui.registerReplacement("transcript", ...),
  which would replace the native renderer (forbidden by the single-chat
  constraint).

  The host's scroll/virtualizer state (chatHasAutoScrolled, chatLastScrollTop,
  transcriptScrollTop, handleTranscriptScroll) is private to the host Lit
  controller. A plugin cannot set it.

  Live measurement (fresh tab, 15:27 IST):
    .chat-thread   scrollTop 0, scrollHeight 3935, clientHeight 518
    rows           3 tail rows at viewport top 3593-3953
    textContent    5,017 chars; innerText "SHOW EARLIER"
    scrollRestoration "auto"
    experiment     setting scrollTop = scrollHeight revealed the 3 rows
                   (scrollTop 3417; row tops 176/296/416)
  The experiment proves the virtualizer responds to scroll, but only through
  host-internal DOM. A helper would depend on host class names and absolute-row
  layout, cannot distinguish a browser-restored or user-chosen position from the
  quirk, and can race host auto-scroll/new-message logic. Not robust/supported.
  Workaround: click any position-rail marker or press End.

  Supersedes the earlier "small Zero helper" suggestion in the 14:52 entry.

  desktop-connection.ts, computer-control.ts, screen-frame-gate.ts,
  desktop-viewer-targets.ts, ComputerPanel.takeover.test.ts). Upstream git status
  clean; no edits.

  Already covered (not duplicated): server-arbitrated global hold, opaque
  server-minted lease, conflict re-read, disconnect invalidation, poll +
  visibility refresh, refused-not-queued, post-release fresh-capture gate,
  in-memory hold, connection-close release.

  Genuine UI-owned gap: leaving /systems (in-app route change) kept the Gateway
  socket open, so a global hold could remain active after its only visible
  release control was removed. The plugin released on connection close, but not
  on route change. OpenMausBot's pattern is "closing the panel, leaving the
  view, or closing the page gives back the hold it took".

  Implemented (behavior pattern only; no source copied; Apache-2.0 reference):
    app/zero-control-ui/src/computer/hold.js
      - createComputerHold().leave(): best-effort hand-back of only this view's
        lease; stops polling, ignores late responses, never renders success,
        never blocks navigation.
      - in-flight take awaited only to release the lease it returns, falling back
        to the hold already held if that take fails; in-flight release not
        duplicated.
      - mountComputerHold(): pagehide listener + leave(); dispose() alone never
        releases (host re-render safety).
      - held-by-you detail says leaving the screen hands control back.
    app/zero-control-ui/src/native-surfaces.js
      - computerSurfaceAction(): route departure = leave; missing Desktop =
        unmount; attached wrapper = reuse; otherwise remount.
      - handBackComputer(): null-safe best-effort.
      - leaving /systems or plugin teardown hands back only this view's hold.
    app/zero-control-ui/test/computer-hold.test.mjs
      - 10 new focused tests (30 hold tests total).

  Deliberately not implemented:
    - RELEASE_SETTLE_MS: no local takeover session to settle; the server
      snapshot is authoritative and generation-guarded.
    - client-minted lease IDs: the plugin mints the opaque lease.
    - keepalive fetch release: the hold is a Gateway RPC; connection-close
      release is the backstop, pagehide is best-effort.
    - RFB allow-list/join/connect deadlines: Zero does not own the transport;
      the host viewer owns it. B3 prerequisite remains.
    - conversation-scoped takeover: Zero's hold is global by design.

  Verification (source/test only):
    - UI suite 81/81 (30 focused hold tests).
    - tsc --noEmit exit 0 (app/tsconfig.json).
    - build generation 16b83c88... (JS 101,066 B sha256 942c11e3...c724;
      CSS 24,114 B sha256 6affcc8d...3004 unchanged).
    - verify-served.mjs pass for the local build.
    - built bundle contains pagehide / leave this screen / hold host; no new
      WebSocket, websockify, EventSource, RTCPeerConnection, noVNC, or RFB.
    - browser check omitted: behavior is in the plugin bundle; checking it would
      use the stale served bundle or require a Gateway reload.

[2026-10-26 22:00 IST] [OpenCode] [OMB-01] [MCP STATUS REVIEW FIXES]
Applied the requested MCP-status review fixes in `app/zero-control-ui/src/shared/connectors.js`.
No live Gateway/config/model/provider/credential/session change; no restart/reload, CLI/MCP/provider/
account/desktop action.

**Transport validation.** Installed `McpServerSchema` accepts only `stdio`, `sse`, `streamable-http`.
Installed `mcp-transport-config` treats a command-bearing entry as stdio; for a remote URL with no
declared transport it falls back to `sse` (`effectiveTransport === "streamable-http" ? ... : "sse"`).
The projection now mirrors that: command → `stdio`; remote omitted transport → `sse`; explicit
`sse`/`streamable-http` preserved; unknown values (including non-string values) and remote
`stdio` → `invalid` (not silently `streamable-http`). URLs/headers remain unexposed.

**Filter validation.** `toolFiltered` is true only when `toolFilter.include` or
`toolFilter.exclude` contains at least one non-empty string. `{}`, empty arrays, empty strings, and
malformed non-array values are false; filter patterns are never returned.

**Tests.** Focused connector regression in `test/mount.test.mjs` now covers: omitted remote
transport → `sse`; explicit `sse`/`streamable-http`; unknown `websocket` and non-string transport →
invalid; remote `stdio` → invalid; command precedence over URL/unknown transport; empty filter
objects/arrays/strings/malformed
values → not filtered; non-empty include and exclude → filtered; redaction of URLs/headers/OAuth/
filter patterns/descriptions/diagnostics.

[2026-10-27 01:35 IST] [OpenCode] [OBS-01] [MCP-STATUS-STYLE REVIEW CORRECTIONS — OBSERVATION ENGINE]
Corrected the OBS-01 implementation before acceptance. No live capture, CUA driver start, Gateway
restart/reload, model/provider/config/credential/session change, install, VLM/YOLO dependency, or
desktop action.

**TASK_BOARD reconciliation.** The pre-implementation OBS-01 row is now marked
`OBS-01 (historical)` and `SUPERSEDED`; the later OBS-01 row is the one authoritative current status.
Historical planning text is preserved, but it no longer claims implementation has not started.

**Error redaction.** Added browser-safe `src/shared/observation-errors.js` and wired About to it.
It strips control characters, Bearer credentials, token/password/secret/authorization assignments,
common `sk-*`/`pk-*`/`ghp_*`/`xox*`/AKIA key shapes, private-key blocks, and URL credentials while
retaining safe categories/context. Product-surface tests render a status error containing all
representative shapes and assert none appears in the DOM while `CUA_CAPTURE_ERROR`/“capture failed”
remain visible.

**Consecutive errors.** `engine.mjs` now keeps `consecutiveErrors` (reset after any successful
capture/start) separate from lifetime `counters.errors`; the stop threshold uses the consecutive
streak only. Tests cover intermittent failure/success, three consecutive failures, stop state, and
recovery via start with lifetime totals retained.

**Retention.** Defaults are implemented and documented: **7-day observation summaries, 30-day
workflow candidates, 10,000-row logical cap, 15-minute bounded prune batch 500, plus one bounded
prune scheduled on start**. This is a logical row bound; it does not cap or guarantee the physical
SQLite file size, and no VACUUM is scheduled. Pruning is a separate timer (not in the capture loop) and bounded by batch;
tests cover expiry, reopen/restart behavior, and row-growth bounds. Raw frames remain absent from
SQLite.

**Private Mode/stop.** Abort signals reach the capture source, worker queue, and UIA/OCR adapter;
stop/Private Mode clear the ring, queue, last signature, and workflow counters, then bound capture,
queued-perception, and source-stop settlement by `cleanupTimeoutMs` (2 s default). A hung capture no
longer blocks shutdown before the timeout race. If work does not settle, status keeps
`cleanupPending:true` / `lastSettlement.timedOut:true`; it does not claim completion. `dispose` defers
SQLite close while any capture/queue/source-stop work remains, and late processFrame work cannot
commit. CUA SDK `getDesktopState` exposes an AbortSignal; native cooperative cancellation is not
assumed, so the bounded settlement is independent. Tests cover in-flight capture abort, queued OCR
abort, adapter abort propagation, no post-Private summary commit, buffer zeroing, final
`private`/`stopped` status, `inFlight:false`, `cleanupPending:false`, and dispose during capture.

**Verification.** UI **106/106** (21 observation tests), mount **35/35**, product-surface **15/15**;
backend selftest **111/111**, bridge **3/3**. Build generation
`4cbb6c340b44ff4666afa6c650fbc4ad0354342aaa22f5b9d77792c0e92cde13`; JS 108,935 B sha256
`4e141fcb…e5b8`; CSS 24,656 B sha256 `ab4dbfcf…10b7a`; `verify-served.mjs` exit 0. Config SHA

[2026-10-27 02:04 IST] [OpenCode] [OBS-01] [BOUNDED SHUTDOWN SETTLEMENT + RETENTION SCOPE CORRECTION]
Focused follow-up: shutdown no longer awaits an un-cancellable capture before its timeout. No live
capture/CUA driver start, Gateway restart/reload, model/provider/config/credential/session change,
install, or VLM/YOLO dependency.

**Settlement.** `settleWork()` now races capture, queued perception, and source stop against a shared
`cleanupTimeoutMs` budget (2 s default). A hung/non-cancellable capture returns control to Stop/
Private Mode/dispose; `lastSettlement` records capture/queue settled/timedOut, and
`cleanupPending` remains true while any capture, source-stop, active worker, or pending job remains.
No observation is committed after Stop/Private Mode/dispose: processFrame checks generation,
privateMode, disposeRequested, and storeClosed before insert.

**Store close.** `dispose()` closes SQLite only when no capture/queue/source-stop work remains.
Otherwise it defers close via settlement watchers; a late worker cannot write after the close, and a
still-running worker keeps the store open. Tests cover hung capture (ignores AbortSignal), capture
settling within the bound, queued perception resolving after Private Mode, late worker after dispose,
adapter abort propagation, and final status.

**CUA cancellation review.** Installed `@trycua/cua-driver` types expose
`getDesktopState(input, { signal: AbortSignal })` and the generated FFI call passes asyncOpts to the
future; native prompt cancellation is not measured. Shutdown therefore relies on the bounded
settlement and generation/store-closed guards, not on the driver aborting promptly.

**Retention scope.** 7-day summaries, 30-day workflow candidates, 10,000-row cap, 15-minute bounded
prune (batch 500), start-time scheduled prune. This is a logical row bound; SQLite physical file size
is not capped or guaranteed to shrink, and no VACUUM is scheduled. Raw frames remain out of SQLite.

**Verification.** UI **106/106** (21 observation tests), mount **35/35**, product-surface **15/15**;
backend selftest **111/111**, bridge **3/3**. Build generation
`4cbb6c340b44ff4666afa6c650fbc4ad0354342aaa22f5b9d77792c0e92cde13`; JS 108,935 B sha256
`4e141fcb…e5b8`; CSS 24,656 B sha256 `ab4dbfcf…10b7a`; `verify-served.mjs` exit 0. Config SHA
`6f051c09…` and model projection `ea75d703…` unchanged; only agent `zero`.

**Limits.** Live capture/OCR accuracy, Windows permissions/capture indicator, CPU/RAM/frame sizes,
latency, and native CUA abort promptness remain unmeasured. No VLM/Whisper weights/runtime, chat Q&A
tool exposure, proactive suggestions, or DPAPI/ACL hardening. OBS-01 READY FOR REVIEW; no
self-acceptance.

Zero sees `zero_screen_context` with suggestions still disabled. Live synthetic-window capture/OCR,
Windows permissions, resource/latency measurement, optional local vision/audio model provisioning,

[2026-10-27 08:30 IST] [OpenCode] [OBS-01] [DURABLE OWNER OPT-IN LIFECYCLE]
Implemented durable owner opt-in for continuous observation without changing live Gateway config. No
live preference-store read/write, CUA driver start, desktop capture/inspection, OCR, model/provider
request, config/credential/session mutation, install, or Gateway restart/reload.

**Store contract.** `observation/preference.mjs` uses the existing plugin keyed store at namespace
`observation-preference`, key `state`, `retention:"retained"`. The record is exactly
`{schemaVersion:1, enabled:boolean, privateMode:boolean, updatedAt:string|null}`. Missing/extra keys,
arrays/non-objects, non-booleans, and unknown schema versions are rejected. No frame, buffer, OCR
text, summary, app/window content, credential, token, path, or observation row can be written; tests
assert the exact key set and scan every record for content/secret shapes. First run performs only a
lookup: `missing` stays disabled with no write.

**Lifecycle.** `observation/lifecycle.mjs` serializes initialize/start/stop/private-on/private-off/
dispose behind one promise chain. Startup resumes only a valid `enabled:true, privateMode:false`
record; persisted Private Mode applies without capture; missing stays disabled; malformed, unreadable,
unavailable storage, and any write failure fail closed to disabled + Private Mode. `start` persists
enabled before starting; `stop` persists disabled before reporting success; `private-on` persists
before stopping capture; `private-off` persists before clearing Private Mode and resumes only when the
persisted enabled value is true. Write failure paths stop/keep-private and return a safe
`observation-preference-unavailable` error without raw store text. Engine bounded settlement,
late-worker generation/store-closed guards, truthful `cleanupPending`, and deferred store close are
delegated unchanged. Dispose blocks later actions and does not delete the owner preference.

**Wiring.** `runtime-core.mjs` opens the retained preference store, starts lifecycle initialization at
plugin registration, and routes `zero.observation.status/control` through the lifecycle. About uses
`preferenceEnabled` for Enable/Stop so a persisted enabled preference is still controllable after a
failed resume. The manifest/UI generation was rebuilt; `zero_screen_context` remains backend-only.

**Verification.** New `test/observation-lifecycle.test.mjs` (12 tests): first-run defaults/no write,
enabled restart recovery, Private Mode restart recovery + conditional resume, persist-before-stop
ordering, malformed/unreadable/unavailable fail-closed with no raw error leak, start/stop/private write
failures, concurrent start/private/stop/private-off serialization, duplicate-start idempotence,
dispose blocking, exact preference keys/no observation content, and runtime-handler status/control
wiring with safe RPC errors. Full UI **123/123** (21 observation + 5 tool + 12 lifecycle), mount
**35/35**; build generation `7f9a2412e7c67d6393b48ff3b49b899721a4f2b362c39612cffff2db0558a6ae`; JS
108,960 B sha256 `e355fbab…bd7c`; CSS 24,656 B sha256 `ab4dbfcf…10b7a`; `verify-served.mjs` exit 0.
Config SHA `6f051c09…` and model projection `ea75d703…` unchanged; only agent `zero`.

**Remaining gates.** Owner-authorized Gateway restart/reload, then confirm the lifecycle is active,
first-run retained store reports `missing` and stays disabled, enabled/Private Mode restart recovery
works, and malformed/unavailable storage stays fail-closed. Synthetic/owned-window capture, UIA/OCR
accuracy, and CPU/RAM/frame/latency measurement remain unmeasured. Live continuity is not claimed.
OBS-01 READY FOR REVIEW; no self-acceptance.

[2026-10-27 08:42 IST] [OpenCode] [OBS-01] [START-FAILURE CORRECTNESS]
Fixed the lifecycle's false-success path when `engine.start()` resolves without a running capture. No
live preference-store read/write, CUA driver start, desktop capture/inspection, OCR, provider/model
request, config/credential/session mutation, install, or Gateway restart/reload.

**Start result inspection.** `startEngine()` now inspects the returned engine status (or `engine.status()`
when none is returned). Only `running:true` with `state:"capturing"` is success. Resolved
`unavailable`, `error`, `stopped`/not-running, `private`, and rejected `engine.start()` calls record a
safe `startFailure {origin, reason, state, message}` with `captureRunning:false` and a constant
`safeError` (`capture-start-unavailable|error|rejected|not-running|private`). Lifecycle status also
sanitizes `lastError`/`lastPruneError` before returning them. No raw engine error/credential text is
returned.

**Preference semantics.** Explicit start still persists `enabled:true` first; a failed start returns
the qualified status with `ok:false`, `startFailed:true`, `captureRunning:false`, and preserves the
enabled preference for a later retry. Startup auto-resume failure records `origin:"startup"`, leaves
the valid enabled record byte-unchanged (no rewrite), and does not claim capture is running; the next
Gateway startup retries. On Private Mode exit with persisted `enabled:true`, a failed resume leaves
Private Mode accurately off and records `origin:"private-off"`; with persisted `enabled:false`,
exiting Private Mode does not start capture. No unsafe fallback start exists.

**About UI.** Explicit start failure now renders "Private Mode is off" plus source-unavailable or
"Capture could not start" text, keeps Disable/Private controls, and never claims capture. The previous
early return for an unavailable source was removed, so Private Mode on/off and the exit toggle remain
accurate when the source is unavailable.

clearing on recovery). Product-surface adds 2 About start-failure cases (explicit start and Private
Mode exit). Full UI **129/129**; mount **35/35**. Build generation

[2026-10-27 08:51 IST] [OpenCode] [OBS-01] [LIFECYCLE/API CONSISTENCY]
Fixed three consistency gaps in the observation lifecycle, Gateway API, and About UI. No live
preference-store read/write, CUA driver start, desktop capture/inspection, OCR, provider/model request,
config/credential/session mutation, install, or Gateway restart/reload.

**Single-start Private Mode exit.** `engine.setPrivateMode(false)` now only clears Private Mode; it
does not call `start()`. The lifecycle is the single owner of resume and calls `startEngine` exactly
once when persisted `enabled` is true. Success returns `captureRunning:true`; failure leaves Private
Mode accurately off, returns `startFailure.origin:"private-off"`, and does not retry implicitly.
With persisted `enabled:false`, exit does not start. A real-engine regression wraps `engine.start`
and asserts exactly one attempt for both success and unavailable-source failure.

**About pending/unknown timeout.** Observation control requests no longer use the generic 5-second
`timeoutCall` path that claimed “no capture was started.” While a control RPC is unresolved, controls
are disabled and duplicate clicks are ignored. At the UI timeout the page reports that the request is
still unresolved/unknown, explicitly says it was not canceled or repeated, and refreshes status. A
late Gateway completion then renders the final status. Status-refresh responses are fenced by request
id/epoch so an older refresh cannot overwrite a newer control result. Tests cover duplicate start
clicks and late completion after timeout, including no repeated control call and no false off claim.

**Recent-history policy.** `zero.observation.recent` now routes through `lifecycle.recent`. Private
Mode returns `ok:false`, `reason:"private-mode"`, `count:0`, empty `observations`; disabled/stopped
returns `reason:"observation-disabled"` with the same empty shape; memory failure returns
`memory-unavailable`. Allowed reads return at most 20 records mapped through the same sanitizer as
`zero_screen_context` (capturedAt/app/window/summary/source/confidence only; no row id, buffer,
database path, or raw secret). Status/control methods are unchanged. A shared regression drives both
`zero.observation.recent` and `zero_screen_context` with the same rows and asserts zero rows in
disabled and Private Mode states, plus sanitized records when allowed.

**Verification.** Lifecycle tests now **18**; product-surface **19**; full UI **133/133**; mount
**35/35**. Build generation
`ccfca335da9c97792faf174aa34a3184e92bdbc920819dd0641d7dccd744e2fa`; JS 110,639 B sha256
`b0f70760dfe10d147d6914c8ee08d6b2403cd01420314be16a1f201b1bae8d69`; CSS 24,656 B sha256
`ab4dbfcf36841567d3b8ca6c5793e459f8deb753a2e011487d65263e2d510b7a`; `verify-served.mjs` exit 0.
Config SHA `6f051c09…` and model projection `ea75d703…` unchanged; only agent `zero`.

**Remaining gates.** Owner-authorized Gateway restart/reload, then live verification of single-attempt
Private Mode exit, blocked recent-history under Private Mode/disabled, About pending/timeout behavior,
